  (`ArcadeLobbyList.partyNamesText`, seat order, ", " between), the summary (`partySummaryText`, "{host} - {seats}":
  "You host" / "{name} hosts", "1 seat open" / "{count} seats open" / "Party full"), then INVITE (focus into the list,
  hidden when full) and LEAVE PARTY (hidden while solo). Every word is an authored field. The board's middle dot is
  " - ": the generators keep prefab text ASCII.
- **A seat** (`PartySlot`): the avatar in its domain ring, no name or role under it (the card's names line carries
  them; the seat keeps its name in a hidden text, which the profile card reads). The open seat is an outlined octagon
  with a +, a tile with no border at rest.
- **The rows** (`PartyOnlineRow`): one fixed 120 x 32 slot at the right holds Invite, Join and Cancel, one at a time
  (`KitOnlineRow.oneVerbPerRow`), each button 120 wide whatever its word. `FriendsListPanel.oneVerbPerRow` picks the
  verb by status (in the menu Invite, in a party Join, in a match Spectate, invited Cancel). Name over status, as the
  build's rows were, not the board's one line.
- **The groups**: JOIN THEIR PARTY, IN THE MENU, IN A MATCH, YOU INVITED, each a header over its own list, shown only
  while it has rows (`KitListCounter`); `FriendsListPanel.onlineGroups` parents each row and moves it when its state
  changes; the empty line shows while all four are empty (`onlineEmptyLine`).
- **Unchanged:** the orb and the drawer (`author_menu_friends_drawer.py --check` OK), the docking.
- **The profile card, fixed the same day** (Shombith's screenshot: one unreadable button, "there is no join button"):
  REPORT and BLOCK (and the three report reasons) were runtime copies of INVITE, and a copy keeps the original's place,
  so INVITE, REPORT and BLOCK drew as one button. The card is now 336 tall with JOIN / INVITE on one row and REPORT /
  BLOCK under them, all authored slots 172 wide (`author_menu_friends_drawer.CARD_ACTIONS`; the reasons take the same
  slots, `FriendProfilePopup.reasonGroups`); the drawer generator fails if two actions shown together overlap. JOIN was
  missing because the one-verb rows hid Join for a pilot alone in the menu: the row still says INVITE, but its state
  keeps Join, so their card offers JOIN and INVITE.
- **Gates:** `author_menu_arcade.py --self-test` (check_card: seats in a row, no bonds, authored words, one-verb rows in
  one shared slot with the fitter off, the four groups wired in order; 8 new planted defects); `author_ui_lattice.py
  --self-test` (its two bond plants retargeted); `PartyTrayTests`. Hand test: `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.

## 108. A mode's preview: FULL SCREEN extends as a tray (2026-10-31, awaiting in-editor approval)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-31):** "during the preview of each game mode when we press fullscreen it goes actual full
screen rather the play area should extend as a tray and the other parts of the UI (right side) should get hidden".
Built straight from the words (no board round): the same tray as the friends drawer's, applied to the preview.

- **What changes** (`ConfigureView` and `ConfigureArenaView`, `author_menu_configure.py`): FULL SCREEN (while flying)
  no longer grows the preview to the whole window and fades everything. The preview's right edge slides out 700 units
  (x 1160 to 1860 at 1920 x 1080, the settings column's right edge) over `fadeSeconds`, and only the right side fades:
  the controls panel, the settings card, the status line and START (`ConfigureCinema.extendAsTray`, `trayExtend`,
  `rightSide`). The header, the briefing under the preview and the fly row stay. The objective bar rides the preview's
  right edge (`followTray`). Pressing it again, or handing the stick back, slides the tray home.
- **No stretched picture.** A live preview texture cannot be swapped mid-session, so the preview renders a texture as
  wide as the open tray from the start (`ModePreviewWindow.textureWidthScale`, 1800 / 1100) and shows the centre that
  fits its frame (`RawImage.uvRect`, `VisibleWidth`). The framed view is the same picture as before; the tray reveals
  more arena at the sides. Cost: the preview renders 1.64x as many pixels while the window is open (the measured
  height is unchanged, capped at 720).
- **Unchanged:** the Maelstrom window (no flying, no FULL SCREEN); every other `ModePreviewWindow` (scale 1).
- **Gates:** `author_menu_configure.py` checks the tray wiring, the texture scale and that the tray ends at the column's
  edge; three new planted defects (FULL SCREEN back to the whole window, a frame-sized texture, START left on screen).
  `PreviewTrayTests`. Hand test: `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.

## 109. The party molecule back, and it grows from the host (2026-10-31, awaiting in-editor approval)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-31, after E shipped):** "we liked the old plasma kind design for the lobby with connected tree but
it locked it for 4 players only do something where it is expandable with the host and its there, also decrease the tone of
the blue a little bit on the bg". Built straight from the words; E's online rows, groups and profile card stay.

- **The molecule is back** in E's party card (`PartyPanel`, `author_menu_arcade.build_panel`): the host in the centre, the
  members bonded to it with the flowing bonds (`Bond_Party.mat`), each seat the avatar in its domain ring over the name
  and role (the Rev 6 `PartySlot`, §45.4). Under it: "You host - 1 seat open", then INVITE and LEAVE PARTY.
- **It grows** (`ArcadeLobbyList.expandingMolecule`, `LayoutMolecule`): the ring shows the seated members plus ONE open
  socket while a seat is free, spread evenly round the host (`MoleculeAngle`: one seat straight below; two left and
  right; three the old triangle), and re-lays every time the party changes; the other slots and their bonds hide. The
  ring is computed from the slots the panel has (`RingSeats`), not from three fixed angles, so a bigger panel grows
  further; the party itself stays 4 (`Docs/MULTIPLAYER_START_HERE.md` §2). The prefab is authored at the full party
  (`MOL_ANGLES` = the computed three-seat ring), and the gate checks the runtime numbers match the generator's.
- **The background's blue is lower**: the drawer window's SurfaceDark wash over its black is 0.45 (was 0.6,
  `author_menu_friends_drawer.WINDOW_TINT`); the pilot record keeps 0.6.
- **Fixed on the way:** E's party card read `CARD_H`, which the arcade game card redefines lower in the same generator, so
  it came out 296 tall instead of its own height; the card's constants are now `PCARD_*`.
- **Gates:** `author_menu_arcade.py --self-test` 36/36 (check_molecule: bonds start at the host and end at their seats,
  each with KitRimMesh; the growing fields; plants "the molecule locked again", the old bond plants);
  `author_menu_friends_drawer.py`, `author_ui_lattice.py` and the other Menu_Main generators `--check` OK;
  `PartyTrayTests` (ring size and angles). Hand test: `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.


## 110. Rev 41 mockups: the Compute console, a run you can see start (2026-10-31, built; awaiting board approval)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-10-31, on the live console at the VM):** "Half of the run does not make sense, can you please
create some mockups of how they look and also the buttons dont work, registering a PC does not work that well there is
no live bar". Measured on the live console before drawing: two runs sent to dragonsweapon at 22:48 were taken by its
agent and stopped 44 s later, and in those 44 s the page showed nothing (a run reports only after its first match, and
a Unity run spends its first minute opening Unity); the VM read "Working" over "Nothing is running"; New run opened on
"This computer" (a line to paste), the wrong default on the live console; the `ai` branch sat 105 commits behind
because nothing ran its sync.

**On the canvas:** the Rev 38 page (`rev38`, the console's page; the canvas holds 40 pages and all 40 are taken, so the
round sits under Rev 38's boards at y 3600). Copies: `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R41*.dc.html`. A states round of one
design, the same kit as Rev 38 (Inter, the dark ground, Jade cyan as the one accent):
- **R41Runs**: the line after Send ("Sent to dragonsweapon. It shows below as Starting within a minute…"), the run as
  **Starting** with the sliding bar (the one motion on the page; off under reduced motion) beside a working run with
  its real bar, a Sent run that says why it waits ("dragonsweapon is on another run: this one starts after it"), the
  Finished table.
- **R41NewRun**: Where lists the computers first with their status and "starts at once" / "starts when free", the best
  one picked (free, else in use with a start allowed, else the team queue), "This computer · paste a line" last; the
  button reads "Start on dragonsweapon now · Thresher in Thresher Time".
- **R41Computers**: **Starting** as a status of its own (the agent took a run, nothing has reported yet) with the run
  and "Unity is opening" under it; Working always names what runs; the row menu's Stop names the run and asks first.
- **R41Notes**: what was wrong, measured, and what each fix changes.

**Built in the same push** (`Tools/Compute/`): the agent posts a heartbeat with `phase: starting` and the unit the
moment it takes a sent run (`Beacon.starting`); `machine_status` returns `starting` for a working heartbeat with no
run; the worker posts the same before a command session; `console.runs` carries the phase; the page shows Starting
(bar, status word, fleet counts, the empty-state line), the waiting reason (`waitReason`), the default place
(`bestPlace`, until the person picks one), the button text, the notice after a send, a confirm before Stop, and a run
shown as Running is not listed again under Sent runs. The hub syncs the `ai` branch every hour with its token
(`Hub.sync_ai`; `Docs/Compute/README.md` section 15) and the AI branch page says when it last did. Gates:
`compute.py self-test` (the agent's new case), the page in Chromium at 1360 and 390 px with a hub stub: Send closes the
sheet, Runs shows the notice and the Starting box, Stop asks and shows Stopping.

**Differs from the build:** nothing; the boards are the built page with the console's data of 2026-10-31. Waits on
Shombith's look at the live console once the VM has pulled.

**Follow-up, built from the words, no board round (Shombith, 2026-11-01, on R41NewRun's sheet):** "we should have the
option to select all intensities and also to make just one match available too. Also latest like serpentine and
other game modes are not here already ... show the game for another pc as well ... so the game collects performance
data using the bug reporting tool", and the sheet showed Now and At a time both lit. Built: the seven steps numbered
the same for every run (1 Vessel, 2 Game mode, 3 Intensity, 4 How many matches, 5 Show the game, 6 Where, 7 When);
Intensity is a set with an "All 4" chip (one run per level, in order); matches go down to 1 with a number field
beside the slider; Show the game is a step for every Where, and a watched run records the game's diagnostics and
sends them with its console errors through the bug ledger (`Docs/Compute/README.md` section 16); When lights one
chip, and At a time shows what follows the time (when free, or at once); Serpentine, Scarab Rally and Brittle Star
Time are on the vessel x mode grid (the trainer's catalog, the regenerated chart). R41NewRun stays the record of the
Where fix; the sheet's later shape is the built page.

## 112. The board becomes the Froglet Design Board, in tabs (2026-11-02)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-02):** "Rename design board artifact from Menu Redesign B to Froglet Design Board. Menu
Redesign should just be a master tab and inside it will be the redesign mockups. We can create new pages using
/design-board-new and new page is opened and the artifact should ask for a name." Done when the board has tabs and
the next round goes on a new page called Menu Redesign C.

- **The canvas is retitled "Froglet Design Board"** (same link, https://claude.ai/artifact/W8DBZaW6pu3L28egCjrKo2,
  version `1793647580-506c`). Nothing was deleted or redrawn: 341 boards, the same files, titles and `order`.
- **Three tabs instead of 40 pages.** The canvas had hit its 40-page cap at Rev 40 (one page per round).
  `design_board.py fold` moved Rev 3-37, Rev 39-40, Start flow, In-game HUD and Menu (with the item boards that had no
  page) into **Menu Redesign** as titled sections, newest first, packed on shelves 36,000 px wide; each section keeps
  its boards' layout and its own title notes, under a title note named for its old page (`rev31`: "Rev 31 - Squads,
  the deck"). The Rev 38 page (Rev 38, 41, 42) became the **Compute Console** tab. **Menu Redesign C** is new and empty;
  the board opens on it.
- **A round is a section, not a page.** `plan` takes `--tab <id>` (or `--new-tab "<name>"`) and puts the round's title
  note `rev<N>` under the tab's last row; round numbers are still claimed by title notes, old page ids and board
  prefixes. `tabs` lists the tabs; `status`, `find` and the copy's README read tabs and sections (`section_of`).
  Self-test: 30 checks, new ones for a round with no tab, a missing tab, the round as a section, `new-tab` (refuses a
  41st tab and a name that exists) and `fold` (page-less boards follow the first page, no frame overlaps after it).
- **`/design-board-new`** (`.claude/skills/design-board-new/SKILL.md`) opens a tab: it asks the person for the name in
  the session when none is given (`/design-board-new <name>` skips the question), runs `new-tab`, publishes the index
  and opens the board on the tab. The canvas's own page bar belongs to the Design type and cannot be changed from here,
  so the name is asked in the session, not by a prompt inside the canvas.
- Doc references to a round's "page" now mean its section on the Menu Redesign tab (same title).

six mockups of each UI are presented and picked; the build follows the pick.

**Read first:** Garrett's Voyage window as built (`94c1050b`, `a1922d78`, open PR froglet-studio/Cosmic-Shore#1717:
SAGA | EPISODES buttons opening modals, the galaxy explorer `VoyageMapGraphic` with the deep zoom and the 20 ly cells,
the edge view `VoyageEdgeGraphic`, the job cards three across that open over each other), `Docs/Voyages/VOYAGE_BOARD.md`,
the slice's words (`voyage_slice.json`), the Ark's squad deck (section 95: three seats x vessel, element, pilot,
artifact = the 12 settings), the arena preview's tray (section 108) and the earlier voyage rounds (Rev 13-17, 25).

**On the canvas:** tab **Menu Redesign C**, round title "Rev 43 - Voyage window ..." (version `1793648453-504c`).
Copies: `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R43*.dc.html`; the generator: `Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r43_voyage_window.py`.
The map and the edge view are the Voyage Board itself rendered at 2x (Neighbourhood tier, the cells, the course from
Tumult to Lantern Reach and its profile), uploaded as `/_blob/b96bc9ce...` and `/_blob/25c96499...`, drawn opaque.
Laid out as one row per option, five screens each: **Voyage** (level 1), **Crew** (level 2), **Story** (level 3),
**Saga**, **Episodes**; the Compare board and the notes on the first row.

- **A - Drawer (recommended):** the card slides in from the right; Ark crew and Story slide over it and leave a spine
  you press to go back. Saga and Episodes use the same frame (art or quest list left, the drawer right). Recommended
  because the map and edge view never move or shrink at any level, the slide matches the arena preview's tray, and
  each level is one press deeper (A Set sail, X Ark crew, Y Story, B back).
- **B - Tray:** the arena preview's tray literally: a narrow card at level 1 that widens over the map at levels 2
  and 3 (the map keeps its left third). Saga full width with the Severing as a strip; Episodes five tall cards.
- **C - Level tabs:** one card, Voyage / Ark crew / Story as tabs on its top (D-pad), the voyage list inside the
  card; the window's tabs a segmented bar. Saga as two equal part cards; Episodes as the old quest tracker.
- **D - Rising sheet:** level 1 a card at the right with the waypoints listed; Ark crew and Story rise from the bottom
  across the width (the 12 settings get the most room, the edge view is covered). Tabs with counts.
- **E - Dossier:** the three levels as numbered steps down one card; the open step fills it. Saga and Episodes as
  steps too.
- **F - Two cards:** Ark crew or Story slides in as a second card between the map and the voyage card; the map narrows,
  the brief stays. Headline tabs (the open tab is the screen's title).

Shared by all six (same content, so the layouts are what differs): level 1 holds the title, the crew's summary, who
found it (Oriel Vance, CITADEL, Hunter), course / sail / intensity / pays / finds / odds, the course cell by cell, the
waypoints, and **Your Ark**: the configured Arks (Skim Wing, Breakers) to choose from, or Configure. Level 2 is the
three seats x vessel, pilot, element, artifact with a preset switcher and Save as Ark. Level 3 (optional) is who brought
it, the place, the news and the water. Saga (Tandava, the Severing) and Episodes (Episode 1's five quests, Episode 2
locked) keep their content.

**Differs from the build:** Saga | Voyages | Episodes become the window's tabs on LB / RB (today SAGA and EPISODES are
buttons opening modals); the map and edge view take the left side, opaque; the three-across grid becomes the voyage
list (a strip on the map or a list in the card); the open card gains the preset (an Ark), a level for the 12 settings
and a story level. Garrett's map and edge graphics stay as they are; only where they sit changes. The words the card
shows that are not authored lines yet (waypoints, odds, the cell-by-cell mix, the story headings) would be new
`VoyageLines` through `voyage_slice.json`, Garrett's file.

Affects: Garrett's Voyage window (`author_voyage_window.py`, `VoyageJobsView`, `VoyageWindowModals`) once a pick is built.

- **The sea lines** (the approved R40E layer) run behind every board. The map and the edge view stay opaque on the
  left; the cards sit on glass so the lines show at their edges.
- **G - Crew's hand:** each voyage is a card a crew member hands you, the open one in front and the others standing
  behind it (LT / RT through them); Ark crew and Story are the back of the same card, turned over with Y.
- **H - Logbook:** the voyage is a ruled page of the Ark's log with its numbers as a ledger; Ark crew is the next page
  (a crew manifest: three seats x vessel, pilot, element, artifact), Story the page after.
- **I - Formation (recommended):** your Ark leads the card, its three hulls in formation, LT / RT through your Arks and
  Configure one press away; the voyage sits under it. Ark crew grows the formation into its three seats with their
  settings. Recommended because the preset becomes the thing you see, and level 2 reads as the Ark opening up rather
  than a new form.
- **J - On the sea:** no card box at all; the levels are columns standing on the sea lines, and a deeper level pushes
  the voyage into a slim column beside it.
- Saga and Quests share one frame in every option (the art large where the map sits, the list in the option's card);
  Episodes is one locked panel. Their contents are unchanged.

**Differs from the build:** as section 113, plus a fourth tab: the quest tracker leaves the Episodes modal for its own
Quests tab, and Episodes becomes a locked tab until episodes ship.

### 116.1 Edit on the Rev 43 and Rev 44 boards: the route lit, and how you move between voyages (2026-11-02)

**Asked (Shombith):** "Can we show the waypoints in the UI in a better animated glowy way and how do we navigate
between actual voyage cards? Please show that edit on the old mockups." So the existing boards were edited in place
(the skill's rule: a board is changed only when the person asks for it), canvas version `1793649920-b902`.

- **Edited:** the level-1 Voyage board of every option, R43AVoyage-R43FVoyage and R44GVoyage-R44JVoyage, plus
  R44ICrew, R44Compare and R44Notes (I's Ark switch moved off LT / RT). Generators: `r43_voyage_window.py`
  (`route_glow`, `map_glow`, `voyage_pager`, `GLOW_CSS`) and `r44_voyage_window.py`.
- **The waypoints, lit:** the route on the card is drawn in the map's own course colour (`#C8FF5A`). A light sweeps
  from the Ark (Tumult, ringed in the Ark's amber) to the destination every 4.2 s, each waypoint flares as the sweep
  passes and stays softly lit, and the destination pulses a ring. The same waypoints light on the map at the same
  moments (their positions measured on the render), so the card and the map read as one route.
- **Moving between voyages:** one answer in every option. A pager of the four voyages the crew found heads the card
  (number, place, minutes, intensity, who found it; the open one outlined with a route-coloured line under it).
  D-pad left / right steps through them, a click picks one, a swipe on the card turns it, and the map follows the open
  voyage. LT / RT stay with the menu's screens and LB / RB with the window's tabs. The voyage strip that sat on the map
  in A, B, E, G, H and I is gone at level 1, so there is one list, not two. In I the Ark switch moved from LT / RT to
  D-pad up / down for the same reason.
- This lifts the quiet rule's "no idle motion" for the route only, because it was asked for; nothing else on the
  boards moves except the sea lines.

## 117. Rev 46 mockups: small voyage cards that slide open, from the card or the HyperSea map (2026-11-02, the direction going ahead; awaiting board approval)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-02, after the Rev 43-44 edits):** "Rather than big cards make smaller cards like arena maybe
and they slide open when we click them or select them in the map. With this design move ahead."

**On the canvas:** tab **Menu Redesign C**, round "Rev 46 - Voyage window: small voyage cards that slide open ..."
(version `1793651532-f418`). Copies are in `Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R46*.dc.html` and the generator is
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r46_voyage_cards.py`. One component draws every board, and each state board starts it in a
different state. All six are interactive:
- **Prototype:** you can press a card or a pin, zoom the map, step through the levels and close the card.
- **Cards:** the four voyages as small cards, with the HyperSea map at Sector showing their four pins.
- **Open:** a card slid open: Lantern Reach at level 1, with the map on its course and the route lit.
- **FromMap:** The Maw's pin pressed on the map. The pin is ringed in the route colour and its card is slid open.
- **Crew:** the open voyage at level 2, Ark crew (the 12 settings).
- **Story:** the open voyage at level 3, the story (optional), for The Maw.

**The design:**
- **The cards.** The four voyages are 2 x 2 arena-style cards on the right of the map. Each shows:
  - the Sector render around its destination as art;
  - the voyage's number and kind;
  - the title;
  - the sail time, the course, the intensity and the pay;
  - who found it.
- **Opening a voyage.** A card (A, or a click), or the voyage's pin on the HyperSea map at Sector, slides the voyage
  open over the cards (0.32 s). From a card, the map moves to the voyage's course. From a pin, the map keeps the pin
  ringed.
- **Moving and closing.** B slides the voyage shut. D-pad left / right steps to the next voyage without closing it.
- **Inside the open voyage,** the three levels stay: the voyage (with Your Ark), Ark crew, Story. Each voyage opens
  onto its own course render and edge profile.
- **Kept from earlier rounds:** the four tabs on LB / RB, the sea lines, and Garrett's seven-tier HyperSea map.

**Where the numbers come from.** Every voyage's words and numbers are the Voyage Board's (its four voyages) and the
slice's. Two exceptions: the summaries for Gallowglass and The Maw extend the board's place lines (the slice has no job
for them), and are marked as proposed copy for Garrett.

**What the build would change** (Garrett's window, `author_voyage_window.py`):
- SAGA / EPISODES become the four tabs.
- The three-across job grid becomes 2 x 2 small cards.
- `VoyageJobCard.Overlay` becomes the slide-in voyage with its three levels.
- A pin on `VoyageMapGraphic` opens the same card.

Affects: Garrett's Voyage window and HyperSea map. Nothing is built yet.

## 119. Built: the Voyage window with Rev 49 C, the route poster (2026-11-02, NOT RUN IN EDITOR)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-02):** "Lets go with C and implement it in the real game."

**What is built.** Rev 49 C (section 118) is the open card, inside the Rev 46 window (section 117): small cards that
slide open. The window has the four tabs of Rev 44 (section 116).
- **The window** (`Tools/Build/author_voyage_window.py`, its second layout, marker `VoyagePages`, rewrites
  `MissionView.prefab`):
  - four tabs, VOYAGES | SAGA | QUESTS | EPISODES, with LB / RB labels. Voyages opens first, and Episodes carries a
    lock, its page reading "Coming soon" over Episode 2.
  - The HyperSea map fills a 1000-wide left column over its edge view (180 high). The voyage cards stand two across
    on the right.
  - The old Saga / Episodes modals and their dims are gone. Their content became the Saga and Quests pages.
- **The tabs** (`VoyageWindowTabs`, new): one page shows at a time, and the picked tab carries its mark. LB / RB step
  and stop at either end (`Step`). While the window is open it claims the bumpers (`HubShoulders`, new), so
  `ScreenSwitcher` does not also slide the hub.
- **The open voyage** (`VoyageOpenCard`, new, built at runtime by `VoyageJobsView` in the card column). It slides in over
  the cards (0.32 s) when a card or its place on the map is pressed. It has three levels:
  - **The voyage:** the chips (course, found by), the kicker, the title and three numbers (course, sail, intensity).
    The route row shows only the two ends, lit, with "n cells - n min" between them. Then come the squad formation
    (hull silhouettes) and its presets beside it from `SquadBook` (pressing one makes it the active squad and saves).
    Its buttons are Configure the squad, Set sail, Ark crew and Story. The pager (< VOYAGE n OF m >) goes round the
    voyages.
  - **Ark crew:** three seats (Leader, Rogue One, Rogue Two), each with four setting buttons: vessel, pilot, element
    and artifact. A press cycles that setting.
    - Edits follow the Ark's own rules: only your own seat in a squad you joined, plus `KeepCardsOwn` and
      `FillPilots`.
    - The edits go to a copy of the squad, as the Ark's own squad editor does (`SquadsPage`'s draft). Save squad
      writes it back and saves; closing the card or picking another squad drops an unsaved change.
  - **Story:** one beat at a time (the news, the place, who brought it, the water), with dots and < > to step. A beat
    with no sentence is left out.
- **The ground** (`VoyageRoutePoster`, new): the map's own painted water, framed on the course at the card's shape
  (`VoyageCardRules.Frame`), with the course lit over it and its two ends marked (the Ark amber, the destination
  lime). It reuses the map's texture (`VoyageMapGraphic.TrySeaView`), because the map's atlas is shared and a second
  live map would fight it.
- **Every word is authored:** 56 new `VoyageLines` fields (`Card*`, `Story*`, `Element*`), set through
  `Docs/Voyages/voyage_slice.json` and `author_voyage_assets.py`.

**Different from the boards:**
- D-pad left / right does not step the story's beats or the voyages. The EventSystem already moves the selection with
  the D-pad, so the < > buttons are selectable instead.
- "Who brought it" names the pilot only; faction display names are not authored yet.
- The Ark escort's card still opens in place, with its difficulty stepper.

**Verified here** (no Unity editor ran):
- `unity_refcompile` player and editor, and `/ship` `gates.sh`: ALL OK.
- `author_voyage_window.py --check` and `--self-test` (eight negative controls; `--check` reads the map column's
  numbers back off the prefab, and a retuned `edgeGap` on disk fails it).
- `author_voyage_assets.py --check` and `--self-test`; `author_menu_mission.py --check` and `--self-test`.
- `check_console_logging.py` and `check_conditional_compilation.py`.
- `VoyageCardRulesTests` (5) ran outside Unity against a small `Mathf` / `Vector2` stand-in, with a negative control:
  an unclamped tab step fails `Tabs_StopAtEitherEnd`.

The hand tests are in `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.

**Fixed in the ship review:**
- An RB that slid the hub onto the Voyage window also stepped it to Saga in the same frame. The tabs now skip the
  frame the window opened on.
- The route poster rebuilt its mesh every frame, even with the card shut. It now rebuilds only when the map repaints
  or its own rect changes.
- Ark crew edited the live squad. It now edits a copy, as the Ark does.

**Follow-ups (open):**
- A Save squad from the card does not publish the squad's public record (`SquadDirectory`); the Ark's
  `SquadsPage` republishes after every read, so it catches up on the next Ark visit. Publishing from here is a
  backend call, Shombith's area.
- Faction display names are not authored, so "who brought it" names the pilot only.
- D-pad stepping of the story's beats and the voyages, if the EventSystem's D-pad navigation can be shared.

## 120. Frogma: the design board renamed, with sections and groups (2026-11-03)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-03):** "rename the /design-board skill and the artifact name to frogma and bring more features
that make it a true design board like grouping and sectioning stuff."

- **Renamed.** The canvas is titled **Frogma** (same link, https://claude.ai/artifact/W8DBZaW6pu3L28egCjrKo2, version
  `1793668484-a986`); the skills are **`/frogma`** (`.claude/skills/frogma/`, helper `frogma.py`, was `/design-board` and
  `design_board.py`) and **`/frogma-new`** (was `/design-board-new`). Both still answer to "design board". This doc, the
  repo copy (`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/`) and the round scripts keep their paths.
- **Sections and groups**, built from the canvas's own shape notes (the Design type's page is fixed and has no artboard
  groups; its Group command groups elements inside one board). Measured in the type's editor: a `rect` note keeps
  x/y/w/h, css (background, border, radius) and page; it is clamped to 8000 px; above 500,000 px2 it is drawn **under**
  the artboards. So a **section** (`sec-<key>`) is a tinted, outlined frame under a round's title and boards, and a
  **group** (`grp-<key>`) a dashed outline with a small title, nested in a section; a board belongs to the innermost
  frame holding its centre. New helper commands: `section`, `group`, `sections`, `move` (a frame with everything in
  it), `arrange` (auto layout in reading order, groups as blocks), `fit`, `ungroup`, `outline` (the layers view:
  tab > section > group > board), `retitle`. `plan` now puts every new round in its own section. `check` fails frames
  that cut across each other, overlapping sections, a shape with text, over 8000 px or small enough to sit over boards.
  `frogma.py --self-test` covers each.
- **On the canvas:** 51 sections were added in one publish, one per round or titled area (Rev 5-49 and the item pages
  that are laid out apart). Eleven areas of the Menu Redesign tab were laid out interleaved before tabs (Rev 3, 4, 7,
  15, In-game HUD's match / vessels / end, Start's parents `ingame` and `menu`, items 0 and 1) and have no section: a
  frame round them would take in other rounds' boards. Giving them one needs their boards moved apart first
  (`arrange` / `move`), which is the board owner's call.
- **Not verified by eye.** The canvas type asks sessions not to render it; the frames were gated offline (`check`) and
  the person sees them on the canvas. Dragging a frame in the canvas moves only the frame; `frogma.py move` moves the
  boards with it.
- **Comments become small agents** (Shombith, 2026-11-03: "leave a comment send to Claude and it quickly changes that
  part, it comment can be a small agent so it happens quickly"). A comment sent to Claude wakes the watching session,
  which only routes it: one background agent per board (`.claude/skills/frogma/comment_agent.md`) reads that board,
  finds the commented element with `frogma.py anchor <board> "<tid>:<path>"` (the Design type's anchor numbering), edits
  that element only, gates it (`check --only`), publishes that one file, replies in the thread and resolves it. Comments
  on different boards run in parallel; the session records the changes in the repo once at the end (`/frogma` §4.5).

## 121. The Froglet UI style guide on Frogma's first tab; every old area in a section (2026-11-03, awaiting decisions D1-D8)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-03):** "give them that ofcourse" (sections for the 11 old areas), then "take garretts color chart
for the game from his PR and lets write UI rules according to that and other UI rules that is required and that should
be shown on the first page of frogma", with the earlier brief: one UI style guide for the whole team, a UI checklist PR
template and CODEOWNERS, the five screens that break it most, and "new themes ... all froglet specific ... so any UI
task, artifact diagrams or presentation, website, codex should all follow these rules".

- **Every board on the Menu Redesign tab is in a section** (version `1793722789-316f`). `frogma.py collect` moved the
  interleaved old areas onto shelves at the tab's bottom, each keeping its inner layout and getting its section: In-game
  HUD (Item 12's four areas, Maelstrom with the end of match), Menu (Items 0-11, each item's notes board joined to its
  item), and Rev 3. Rev 4, 7 and 15 got theirs in place once the strays left. 0 boards outside a section.
- **The Style Guide tab is Frogma's first page and the board opens on it** (version `1793723216-5530`): ten boards
  (`SG*.dc.html`) drawn by `Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/style_guide_boards.py` from the tokens: start here, colour (the six
  families), themes, type, shape and space, buttons and states, cards / windows / toasts, motion, do and don't, the UI
  checklist and the five screens.
- **The guide in words:** `Docs/UI_STYLE_GUIDE.md`. **Tokens:** `Docs/UI/froglet.css` and `froglet-tokens.json`, written
  from `UITheme.asset`, `OriginalColorSetSO` and `colour_domains.json` by `Tools/Build/froglet_style.py` (`--check`).
  Themes: Deep Sea (the game's own, default) and Shore (its light twin, each family re-picked inside itself at 4.5:1).
- **Wired into PRs:** `.github/pull_request_template.md` (UI checklist section), `.github/workflows/ui-checklist.yml` +
  `Tools/Build/check_ui_pr.py`, and CODEOWNERS for the UI paths (Shombith).
- **Found while writing it** (decisions in the guide's section 13): the selection blue sits in the forbidden band (D3);
  five STYLE_FOUNDATION light / dark domain variants fall outside their family (D4); the UI domain tokens and the game
  palette disagree (D2); STYLE_FOUNDATION section 3 still says focus is CTA green (D5).
- `/frogma-show` opens the board in one call.
- **Decisions (Shombith, 2026-11-05)**, recorded in the guide's section 13: D1 Deep Sea and Shore; D2 the domains as the
  colour board shows them; D3 Jade is what you can claim and reclaim (selection, focus, hull picks, friends) and Ruby is
  no way back (Ready); D4 the whole colour chart on the Style Guide (`SGChart`, the wheel from
  `Docs/UI/colour-wheel.png` and each family's shades) with in-family replacements for STYLE_FOUNDATION's five
  out-of-family variants; D5 CTA always lime; D6 phones on their own 800 x 450 scale; D7 the UI checklist required;
  D8 a prompt for the codex re-skin. The Style Guide republished as version `1793891384-6434` (11 boards).

  the Profile's veil is drawn in the equipped set.

  over the Profile (APPLY / CANCEL straddling its foot). The smallest change to the built screen.
- **R51C:** a shelf of four large cards under the pilot card, each its own preview; the record moves below.
- **R51Loading:** the cell-change veil (`EnvironmentLoadVeil`, section 47.4) with its words for a set change: CHANGING
  COLOUR SET, the set's name, old -> new rim and face per domain, the omni crystal's hex filling with the new set over
  the old, and the four steps (mixing the palette, repainting prisms, recolouring vessels and trails, opening the cell).
- **R51Applied:** back in the Profile with Dart Frog equipped, the toast "Colour set applied", the cell behind repainted.
- **R51Locked, R51Unlock (Later, once quests are planned):** a set a quest has not given yet (grey, the quest's goals,
  OPEN VOYAGE), and the Voyage window's claim handing over a set (USE IT NOW repaints at once, LATER leaves it in the
  Profile). Quest 4 / Maelstrom is an example, not a plan.
- **R51Notes:** the options, unlocked-for-now, what the repaint does in Unity, decisions.

A is recommended: one more tab on the screen that is built, nothing else moves, and the preview is large enough to judge
a palette; it is the Voyage window's LB / RB tab pattern.

**Differs from the build:** the Profile has no colour sets today, and the game has one palette, chosen by the slot on
`ThemeManagerDataContainer.asset`. Building a pick means: a per-player chosen set (saved like the other profile
settings); `ThemeManager` rebuilding its four domain material sets and handing the set to `GameToastAPI` and `PrismLit`
again (today it does that once, at Awake); and reloading the menu cell under the existing veil, since prisms take
their material when they spawn. The hull materials, card art, studios and the launcher still read Photic
(`Docs/PALETTE.md` section 1), so a set is not a full repaint until they have a per-set path.

**Decisions waiting:** which option; the word ("Colour sets", Garrett's, since "theme" is the style guide's Deep Sea /
Shore); later, which quests give which sets; Dart Frog is on the paid track in `colour_sets.json` (Garrett's call);
whether the UI's domain colours follow the set (style guide D2 says a domain is the live palette).

**Picked (Shombith, 2026-11-05):** "go with A, build it in Unity". Built in section 123.

## 123. Built: Rev 51 A, the Profile's Colour sets tab and the repaint (2026-11-05, NOT RUN IN EDITOR)

Shombith: "go with A, build it in Unity". Every set is unlocked (the quests are not planned yet). Compiled against the
Unity reference assemblies (`Tools/Build/unity_refcompile`, 0 errors in project code); not opened in the editor.

**The switch** (the one palette slot stays the truth):
- `ColorSetCatalogSO` (`Assets/_SO_Assets/Color Palettes/ColorSetCatalog.asset`, written by
  `Tools/Build/author_colour_set_catalog.py`, `--check` / `--self-test`): one entry per set, with an id, its
  `SO_ColorSet`, the name and line the Profile shows (authored in the asset), and `unlocked` (all on).
  `ThemeManagerDataContainer.Catalog` points at it.
- `ColorSetPreference`: the pick is saved on the device (PlayerPrefs). A set changes how this player sees the game,
  nothing anyone else sees.
- `ThemeManager`: at Awake it puts the saved pick (if it is in the catalog and unlocked) into
  `ThemeManagerDataContainer.ColorSet`, then builds the four domain material sets as before. `ApplyColorSet` puts a
  new set in the slot and repaints the SAME material instances in place (`PaintDomainMaterialSet`, split out of
  `GenerateDomainMaterialSet`), hands the set to `GameToastAPI` and `PrismLit` again, saves the pick and raises
  `ColorSetApplied`. Live prisms change colour without a rebuild because they draw with those shared materials.
  `OnDestroy` puts the authored palette back in the slot, so an editor play session never leaves the asset pointing at
  another set.
- The four menu views that held a palette of their own (`CodexScreen`, `ConfigureSeatPedestal`, `KitDomainTile`,
  `SquadsPage`) now paint with `ThemeManager.LiveOr(theirs)`.

**The loading screen:** `EnvironmentLoadVeil.ShowColourSetChange(name, line, repaint)`, the cell-change veil in a
colour-set mode: "Changing colour set", the set's name and line, and four steps from
`EnvironmentLoadVeilContentSO.colourSetSteps` (Mixing the palette, Repainting prisms, Recolouring vessels and trails,
Opening the cell) plus `colourSetTip`. The repaint runs once the screen is opaque; the steps light 0.3 s apart and the
veil lifts. No load gate is taken. Words authored by `Tools/Build/author_load_veil_content.py` (its checks now hold the
four colour-set steps too).

**The Profile** (`Tools/Build/author_menu_profile.py`, `ProfileView.prefab`):
- Two word tabs, RECORD and COLOUR SETS (`KitTextTab`), in the header row beside "Your pilot". **Departure from the
  board:** the board drew them under the pilot card with a third tab, Audio Studio. In the header the approved record
  keeps every position, and Audio Studio stays on the Record page where Rev 13 put it. A third tab is one generator
  change if wanted.
- `ProfileColorSetsPanel` on the `ColourSets` layer over the record's panels: three set cards (name, line, the four
  domains' prism swatches, Equipped with the lime tick / Owned / "Unlocks with a quest"), the picked set's preview
  (Jade, Ruby, Gold, Blue at Prism, Shielded, Super-shielded, plus the vessel colour; HDR shown at its hue), and APPLY
  (Cta) straddling the preview's foot, hidden while the shown set is equipped. Opening the Profile again returns to
  Record.

**Not built:** the board's per-card cell pictures and big cell preview (swatches stand in), Clear Sight (no asset
yet), LB / RB on the tabs (the picker uses them), and the Later boards (quest locks and the claim window).

**Not repainted by a switch:** what reads Photic by path (hull materials, card art, the studios, the launcher accents;
Docs/PALETTE.md section 1), a vessel's own material copies until it respawns, and a live crystal's tint until it next
re-tints. Hand test: `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.


## 124. Fix: APPLY reaches the live world; LB / RB on the Profile's tabs (2026-11-05, NOT RUN IN EDITOR)

Shombith, on section 123 as built: "I apply it but the color of the game does not change at all, I have to restart the
game in order for it to be applied", and "lb rb stuff is missing".

- **Why nothing changed.** `ThemeManager.ApplyColorSet` repainted the domain materials in place, but prisms draw through
  Entities Graphics and each prism entity COPIES its bright, dark and spread out of its material when it is created
  (`PrismRenderService.Create`). The copies kept the old set until every prism respawned, which is what a restart did.
  Crystals do the same into their own property blocks (`Crystal.ApplyColorSetTint`).
- **The fix.** `ApplyColorSet` now calls `PrismRenderService.RestampColors(<the repainted materials>)`, one pass over the
  prism entities that re-reads the three colours for every entity on a repainted material, and `Crystal.RetintAll()`.
  Both run behind the veil. Vessels and settled MeshRenderer prisms (the no-Entities-Graphics path) draw the shared
  material and already changed. A prism its owner shades (`ApplyColorShade`) takes the raw new colours until its next
  material sync.
- **LB / RB.** The tab row has the LB and RB keycaps the board drew (`KitKeycap`), and `ProfileColorSetsPanel` steps
  between the tabs on the pad's shoulders while the Profile is up, as the Codex and the Hangar step theirs. It stands
  down while the favourite-pilot picker is open (it owns LB / RB; `ProfileRecordWindow.IsPickerOpen`) or a window over
  the Profile has the pad.
- Compiled against the Unity reference assemblies (0 errors in project code); the hand test in
  `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md` now expects the colours to change the moment the veil lifts.

## 126. Built: Rev 54 A, the Showroom - the Colour sets tab is a window onto the real cell (2026-11-05, NOT RUN IN EDITOR)

Shombith: "go with A, build it in Unity". It replaces section 123's cards and preview panel. The tabs, LB / RB, the
catalog, the veil's colour-set mode and section 124's re-stamp stay.

- **The world is the real cell.** While Colour sets shows, `ProfileColorSetsPanel` switches off the Profile's veil and
  its sea lines, hides the pilot card and hides the record without closing it (`ProfileRecordWindow.SetHidden`), so the
  menu cell is seen through a framed window with a fade under the words. No render texture and no second scene.
- **Browsing previews on the cell.** This answers section 125's open question; the board's notes recommended it.
  Picking a set, pressing < or >, or moving the pad onto a tile calls `ThemeManager.PreviewColorSet`, which repaints
  in place and re-stamps without saving. APPLY raises the cell-change veil, then `ApplyColorSet` saves the pick and
  makes it `Equipped`. If another load holds the screen, APPLY wears the set at once. Leaving the tab or the Profile
  without applying puts the equipped set back: `ProfileRecordWindow` now raises `Opened` and `Closed`.
- **On the cell:**
  - "2 of 3" and "Wearing Nocturne" (authored templates);
  - "Only you see your colour set";
  - "Colour set", the set's name at 88 (the board's size, past the type scale's Display 48 on purpose), its line, and
    the four domains' rim and face swatches with their pigment words;
  - the rail: <, three set tiles (the four rim-over-face swatches, the name, the lime tick on the worn set), >;
  - APPLY (Cta), or Equipped when the set shown is the one worn.
- **Pigment words** are authored in the catalog (`ColorSetCatalogSO.Entry.pigments`; `author_colour_set_catalog.py`
  added them to the asset once, and its checks hold four per set).
- **Departures from the board:** the tabs stay in the header row (as built in section 123). Audio Studio stays on
  Record; that decision is still open. The thumbnails are swatch tiles, not pictures of the cell.
- Compiled against the Unity reference assemblies; `author_menu_profile.py` and `author_colour_set_catalog.py` pass
  `--check` and `--self-test`. Hand test: `Docs/UNITY_VERIFICATION_CHECKLIST.md`.

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-05/06):** "None of it, Remove this completely. We want to inside of the ark in more of a 3d
view with maybe a couch and prisms but no characters or vessels and cards can be on top of that. Create a new modernized

**The game it is built from.** Amoebius could not compile the game since about Oct 29; this branch fills the 72 missing
Unity members (`Port/`, commit `fix(amoebius): the live game compiles and runs again`), and the screenshots were taken in
it: the menu's live cell (HUD hidden with `set UI_Refactored Canvas enabled false`), prisms up close, the whole cell, the
Rampage arena (the Compare board). What they showed: a prism is a near-black block whose bevelled edges carry a bright
fresnel rim in its domain colour; the cell is lattice tubes of gridded blocks, lattice-disc flora and block trails in blue
haze; the live palette is Garrett's Nocturne.

**The room is the real Ark** (`ArkReverse.fbx`, `Docs/VesselModels/ArkReverse.md`): its **hangar** in the prow, between
the jaws, rendered from the model itself (`Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r59_ark_hangar_render.py`, Blender 5, seeded):
- the model's bay of arched frames, its three launch pads for the three escorts (`hangar.slot.0..2`, one per seat), the
  floor lights, the glowing back wall and the tunnel that curves down through the keel, and the clam-jaw doors opened with
  the model's own `hangaropen` take; the outside camera shows the prow, the jaws and the neon-framed mouth;
- in the game's ship materials (`vessel_colors.py`: the gunmetal body, glass, the engine ember) with the domain slot in
  the squad's domain (Nocturne's `ShipColor1` / `ShipColor2`), drawn with VesselGraph's read;
- the hull's prisms in the model's own sockets (`socket.*` bones, 108), each socket's kind at its real size (gills and
  danger blades 3.6 x 1.6 x 9, octahedra 4.8, stellations 6.6), in Nocturne's block colours.

Dressed with the game's own pieces, raw and noisy: the squad's prisms hover over the pads (no couch; the third seat is
KEPT for a friend, its prism a dark outline until they come aboard); the HyperSea board between the arches (the Voyage
Board's own Sector chart, the Ark at Tumult and its route, rendered from `Docs/Voyages/VoyageBoard/voyage-board.html` at
2x with its chrome hidden; the galaxy on the opposite wall); heaps of prism debris along the walls; danger thickets at the
mouth; hanging block chains; junk in the haze; out of the open mouth, the cell (the game's own picture of it) with lattice
tubes and a block trail. Three close cameras with depth of field (in the hangar, at the board, from the cell) x three
domains, graded darker (`r59_ark_lobby_grade.py`).

**On the canvas:** tab **Menu Redesign A** (id `menu-redesign-c`), round "Rev 59 - The Ark's lobby ...". Copies:

(no way back), the kept seat's INVITE is Lime (a call to action), elements have no colour. Every board is interactive: the

## 136. Rev 61 mockups: the Ark's lobby, filmed - the real Ark and the squad's real vessels, no cartoon (2026-11-06; C and E liked and kept, followed up in section 139)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-06, on Rev 59):** "I liked D and in C the map ... can you also show the vessels beside the ark
and then another inside ... current one looks like they are from a cartoon please we need to fix that first. Cannot be
looking like a cartoon."

**Why Rev 59 read as a cartoon, and what changed.** Rev 59 (section 134) drew every surface self-lit under one strong blue
fill light, and the Standard view transform clipped each glow to a flat colour, so the hull read as black with neon
outlines and the debris as candy. Rev 61 films the same pieces (`Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r61_ark_real_render.py`,
Blender 5, Cycles, seeded):
- AgX tone mapping (light rolls off instead of clipping); a near-black world whose only sky is the cell (the Amoebius
  screenshot as the environment); every light motivated: the cell through the open mouth, strip lights in the arches,
  the pads lit from below in the squad's domain, the back wall's ember, a cold sun and a rim outside;
- the hull lit, not self-lit: rectangular panels with seams and sub-plates on a 3D grid, each panel its own shade,
  grime, gloss that wanders, scratches (procedural, no textures); the domain slot as the hull's running lights in the
  squad's domain (Nocturne `ShipColor2`), the jets and pads at rest, glass as glass;
- the prisms as the game reads them, but lit (`r61_prismkit.py`): glossy black faces that reflect the room, the rim
  colour caught on their bevelled edges, the fresnel kept faint; the spent prisms in the heaps mostly dead and dark;
- haze in the hangar, depth of field; in the grade (`r61_ark_lobby_grade.py`) bloom, lateral chromatic fringing, a
  vignette and film grain.

**The vessels.** The squad's own models at their real sizes, in the squad's domain: the leader's Sparrow
(`SparrowModel1.fbx`, 12.5 u) and Rogue One's Dolphin (`dolphin_shapekey_with_animations.fbx`, 5.3 u; its nose is at -y
after import, so it is turned). Inside they stand on their launch pads (head on the pad, tail toward the door, as
`ArkReverse.md` places `hangar.slot.0..2`); the third pad is kept for Mothwing, empty but for a dark prism outline.
Outside they fly beside the Ark, banked to show their backs, each laying the game's block trail.

**On the canvas:** tab **Menu Redesign A** (id `menu-redesign-c`), round "Rev 61 - The Ark's lobby, filmed ...". Copies:
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/R61*.dc.html`; the boards' generator `r61_ark_lobby_boards.py`, the uploaded ids

Nocturne, the Jade selection ring, Ruby TAKE INTO, Lime INVITE, elements without colour); the domain chips now relight
the Ark, the vessels' trim and their trails.

- **A - Beside the Ark (recommended):** Rev 59 D, filmed: outside before the prow, the Ark's mouth open on the lit hangar,
  the Sparrow and the Dolphin flying beside it; the seats in a column on the left. Recommended because it is the view

- **B - The leader, close:** on the Sparrow's wing, the Ark and the Dolphin beyond; the seats on the right.

- **F - Beside the Ark, with the map:** A plus the Voyage Board's Sector chart as a glass panel (the Ark at Tumult).

**Differs from the build:** today the Squads tab is the Rev 31 deck on the menu's sea (section 95). This puts the Ark and
its escorts behind it: baked backdrops per domain (these renders) or a small live scene with `ArkReverse` and the seats'

## 139. Rev 62 mockups: the Ark's lobby by the game's own light - C and E kept, portholes, no vessels (2026-11-06, awaiting a pick)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-06, on Rev 61):** "I like C and E. Keep both and follow that style design and create more

and D (which Rev 61 replaced), were taken off the canvas to make room under its 512-file cap; their copies stay in
`Docs/MenuRedesignB/board/`.

**What changed** (`Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r62_ark_ports_render.py`, from r61's, Blender 5, Cycles, seeded):
- **No light rig.** Every lamp of Rev 61 is gone. The room is lit only by what the game has: the cell (its picture as
  the sky) coming in through the open mouth and the portholes, and the game's own glow, which is the prisms' rims, the
  hull's domain trim and the HyperSea board's screen. The same AgX view, lit hull panels and prism read as Rev 61.

  tunnel; none behind the HyperSea board or the galaxy. Each is placed on the model's real wall (a ray cast from the
  bay's axis onto `ArkReverse`). It is a heavy ring of the hull's metal with eight bolts, and its pane is a portal: it
  shows the cell in the direction you look through it, so it has true parallax, and its light comes into the room.

  render put a floating prism with a ring of shards over each taken pad and an outline over the kept one; Shombith:
  "What are these, remove these." They were removed and every frame rendered again.)

**On the canvas:** tab **Menu Redesign A** (id `menu-redesign-c`), round "Rev 62 - The Ark's lobby by the game's own
light ...", beside Rev 61 C and E. Six option boards (the canvas is at its file cap; the generator
`r62_ark_lobby_boards.py` writes Compare and Notes too with `--all`); the uploaded ids are in `r62_blobs.json`. Six
views x three domains. Cards, colours and interaction as Rev 61 (style guide: the domain in Nocturne, the Jade
selection ring, Ruby TAKE INTO, Lime INVITE, elements without colour).

- **A - The pads (recommended):** Rev 61 C by the game's light: from the door down the bare pads, the
  portholes and the back wall's tunnel; seats on the right. Recommended because it is the view picked, now by the
  game's own light.
- **B - The map wall:** Rev 61 E by the game's light: the HyperSea board between portholes; seats on the right.
- **C - Down the bay:** from the back wall, high: the bay to the open mouth, portholes down both walls.
- **D - The porthole row:** along the starboard wall, its portholes receding with the cell outside; seats on the left.
- **E - Across the bay:** from the starboard corner, the pads before the board.
- **F - At a porthole:** close at the glass, the cell outside, the room dark around it.

**Is it animated? (Shombith asked.)** Not on the board: each view is a still render per domain, and the only motion is
the cross-fade when a domain chip is pressed. Built as a live scene from `ArkReverse` and the game's prisms, these could
move, all from things the game already does: the cell moving past the portholes and the open mouth (the menu
already runs a live cell behind it), the prisms' rims pulsing, haze drifting through the porthole light, the clam
doors opening and closing (the `hangaropen` take), and the domain trim relighting when the domain changes. A baked backdrop cannot do these; a short looping clip could fake some of
them.

**Differs from the build:** today the Squads tab is the Rev 31 deck on the menu's sea (section 95). This puts the Ark's
hangar behind it by the game's own light: baked backdrops per domain (these renders) or the `ArkReverse` hangar as a
small scene with the game's real prisms, lit only by the cell and the game's materials. Seat data, Make, Join, Edit and
Take into are unchanged; the kept seat is new. The build waits on a pick.

Affects: Shombith's Ark tab (`author_menu_ark.py`, `SquadsPage`) once a pick is built; Garrett's Ark model (rendered
only), Voyage Board (rendered only) and Nocturne palette (read only).

## 140. Rev 64 mockup: the Ark's lobby as a live, animated scene (2026-11-06, for review)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-06, on Rev 62):** "Is this bg animated, what are its features?" and then "make a separate
mockup for live animated scene".

**What it is.** One board (`R64Live`) whose backdrop is a seamless animated loop of Rev 62's "Down the bay" view
(section 139): the real `ArkReverse` hangar lit only by the cell and the game's own glow, the portholes, the bare pads.
It shows what the Squads tab would look like if the hangar were a live scene rather than a baked still. Everything
that moves is something the game already does:
- **the cell** outside drifts: the sky seen through the portholes and the open mouth turns slowly, and the lattice
  outside sways past the mouth;
- **the prisms' rims pulse**, each material on its own beat (the heaps, the hull's sockets, the danger blades);
- **haze** rolls through the porthole light (4D noise moved round a circle, so the loop closes without a seam);
- **the clam doors** shut and open again: the model's own `hangaropen` take, eased from open to rest and back, so
  the mouth's light goes and returns;
- **the camera** breathes a little.

Rendered with `Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r64_ark_live_render.py` (built on r62's scene; Blender 5, Cycles, seeded,
60 frames at 1280x720, graded with r62's grade) and assembled into an animated WebP played at 10 fps (a
6-second loop). The board (`r64_ark_live_board.py`) puts Rev 62's seat cards on top, with a panel listing what
moves. The loop is rendered in Jade; the Ruby and Gold chips show Rev 62's stills of the same view (a loop per domain
would triple the render). Uploaded ids: `r64_blobs.json`.

**For the build:** this is the "live scene" path of section 139: `ArkReverse`'s hangar as a small scene with the game's
real prisms and cell, lit only by them. Each moving part maps to existing game systems: the menu's live cell, the
prism rim shader, a volume or particle haze, the Ark rig's `hangaropen` take (which `ArkModel` already plays), and a
gentle camera sway. A pre-rendered loop (this file) is the cheaper fallback: a video backdrop per domain.

Affects: Shombith's Ark tab (`author_menu_ark.py`, `SquadsPage`) once a path is picked; Garrett's Ark model (rendered
only, its `hangaropen` take played).

## 187. Rev 67 mockups: the Ark screen inside the Ark, every tab its own room, animated, in every colour set (2026-11-07, for review)

**Asked (Shombith, 2026-11-06):** Rev 61 C is the Ark's final design ("Keep this 61 C style and lighting, this is
great"), E liked too. "When we select Ark from the menu it's basically inside the Ark": the Converter "a big window
from inside the Ark or a place inside where the conversion happens in 3D", the player's elements "in 3D lying around
inside the converter view, and then when pressed convert they actually convert"; Artifacts "a 2D UI but also a 3D
artifact cabinet"; Decor the same but locked; "everything should be animated"; mockups "for all colour sets like
Photic, Dart Frog and Nocturne"; the Ark's mockups moved to a new tab, the old designs kept. On Rev 61 C (a comment
and drawings on the board): "the 3 vessels should line up and everything 3D ... animate this, make ripples in the
tunnel behind them, make the galaxy map vessel animated". Asked about the drawings: the two tall shapes are pilots
walking around (later, once this is done: the captains you have selected walking the hangar). "There shouldn't be any
reserved [seat]: all the squad mates should show up; if they are offline the vessel lighting can be off, but anyone
can take their place in the squad, and at that time that place will be empty; upon changing vessels it should update."

**On the canvas:** a new tab **Ark** (id `ark`), with Rev 61 C and E, Rev 62 and Rev 64 moved into it (kept as they
were); round "Rev 67 - The Ark screen inside the Ark ...": ONE interactive board, `R67Ark`, the Ark screen itself - its tab row
(LB / RB or a click) switches between the four rooms, each with its own backdrop (one board, because the canvas is at
its 512-file cap and the old designs are kept). Each backdrop is a
seamless animated loop (animated WebP, 10 fps) rendered from `ArkReverse` in Rev 61 C's style and lighting
(`Docs/MenuRedesignB/rounds/r67_ark_tabs_render.py`, Blender 5, Cycles; `r67_assemble.py` grades with Rev 61 C's
grade and encodes). A dashed **Mockup - colour set** switch (not part of the game's UI) swaps every backdrop between
**Nocturne, Photic and Dart Frog**: the hull's domain trim, the pads' light and every prism (the hull's sockets, the
heaps, the danger blades, the trails) take that set's colours, read from `Assets/_SO_Assets/Color Palettes/`.

- **Squads tab:** the squad's three vessels lined up across the bay, facing you, in the left two-thirds of
  the frame (the drawing's three loops): Halcyon's Dolphin, your Sparrow, Mothwing's Serpent. Mothwing is offline:
  their Serpent is powered down (no domain trim, no engine, no pad light), still standing in the line. No reserved
  seat: the offline seat's card offers **Open the seat** (Lime), which empties it ("the pad stands empty until someone
  takes it"); your card's **Change vessel** updates your pad. Ripples run out of the back wall's tunnel through the
  hull's domain trim; on the HyperSea map a vessel marker travels the route from the Ark at Tumult toward Tollmoor
  and back, the Ark's ring breathing; the online vessels hover and their engines breathe; rims pulse; haze drifts.
- **Every other tab is its own room** (Shombith, mid-round: "all screens have different rooms"; and of the first converter
  render, where the crystals lay in piles on the hangar floor: "don't keep the crystals like this, only in the
  converter screen" - a separate converter room). The rooms are built in the hangar's own plating, ribs, domain trim,
  socket prisms, strip lights and haze; the hangar keeps no crystals.
- **Converter tab: from inside the Ark, down the pier, Garrett's combine out in the cell** (Shombith, on the board,
  2026-11-12: "I don't want a room, I want the old animation that Garrett had and the same setup ... an open part of
  the Ark where this is happening outside in the cell"; then, of a first pass that showed the combine on its own:
  "the camera should be inside the Ark and the crystals outside on an elongated platform, we should be able to see the
  cell toybox for sure, and the conversion happens in the middle"). The camera stands at the back of the hangar and
  looks out of the open mouth (the clam doors swung on past their open pose, `hangar.door.T` / `.B` at -150 / 150
  degrees, so the mouth is a clear frame) down a pier that carries the hangar's floor 82 m out into the cell: the
  hull's plating on girders, the domain's trim down both edges, posts with the squad's prisms for lamps, and a round
  deck in its middle ringed in the domain. Beyond it the cell: the in-game cell picture and its lattice swaying
  (`r67_ark_tabs_render.py`'s `pier` scene). Over the round deck Garrett's own animation plays, captured frame by
  frame from his Omni Combine Lab (`Docs/Studios/OmniCombineLab.html`, the source `OmniCombinePlayer` is a line-for-line
  port of) with its shipped settings, only its camera lowered (pitch 0.12) to match a view from inside the hangar:
  at rest the four crystals (Space, Charge, Mass, Time) each do their effect; **Convert** plays his combine to the
  Omni (the morph, 10.7 s), the recipe merges each element in turn over it, and the Omni stays over the deck. The
  crystals light the deck lime and bloom into the cell (`r67_omni_capture.cjs` captures, `r67_comp_converter.py` lays
  it over the pier loop). The converter's panels (the recipe, the progress bar, Convert again) are the board's own,
  as someone reworked them on the canvas; only the backdrop changed, now per colour set. The reactor room is retired.
- **Artifacts tab: open to the cell, not a closed room** (Shombith, 2026-11-12: "the artifact screen should not be
  a closed Ark room"): its backdrop is the Converter's view from inside the hangar out of the open mouth, without the
  crystals; the list and the selected artifact's card (its real description, who holds it, **Give to a seat**) sit on top.
  The vault render is retired; the 3D cabinet is for the live scene in the game.
- **Decor tab: locked completely** (Shombith, 2026-11-12: "there is no decor scene, we do it later"): no screen and no
  backdrop; the tab shows its lock, a press says it opens later, and LB / RB cycle Converter, Squads and Artifacts only.
- **No more renders for this round** (Shombith, 2026-11-12: "you do not need to render more, we can do it live on the
  game"): the Converter and Artifacts backdrops are one still frame of the `pier` scene (Nocturne, shown for every colour
  set) with Garrett's combine as the only motion; the live scene in the game is the target.

**Not in this round:** the pilots walking the hangar (Shombith: later); the Breakers squad's own vessels (its
backdrop stays Skim Wing's, lit in its own domain).
After a conversion the converter returns to the four crystals at rest (the loop).

**For the build:** this is the live-scene path: `ArkReverse`'s hangar for the Squads and a room for Artifacts and Decor, the squad's real hulls on the floor (powered down when offline), the Converter as
`OmniCombinePlayer` itself (unchanged) placed over a pier that runs out of the hangar's mouth into the cell, the
menu camera inside the hangar, the vault and the decor antechamber as set pieces, the map's marker on the
Voyage Board's route, and the tunnel's ripples as a trim shader parameter. The renders are the reference for the look.

Affects: Shombith's Ark screen (`author_menu_ark.py`, `ArkView`, `SquadsPage`, `ElementalConverterView`) once a
build is picked; Garrett's Ark model, the Sparrow, Dolphin and Serpent models, the crystal models (rendered only); the
colour sets and the artifacts (read only).

**Domain (Shombith, on the board, 2026-11-07: "changing domain does not affect the environment"):** the Squads
domain chips now relight the hangar itself - the hull's trim and the tunnel's ripples, the pads' light, the squad's
prisms, the vessels' domain trim and the map's marker - from loops rendered in Jade, Ruby and Gold for every colour set
(`ARK_DOMAIN`; `r67_map_frames.py ... <domain>`).

**Status (2026-11-07):** complete on the board: all four rooms' loops in Nocturne, Photic and Dart Frog, and the
Converter's conversion in all three, and the hangar in all three domains (21 animated WebPs, ids in `r67_blobs.json`). Rendered at 960 x 540, 10 samples,
32-frame loops and a 40-frame conversion at 10 fps; `r67_map_frames.py` makes the HyperSea map's marker frames.
